Heavy footsteps echoed behind him, and he felt Korric's large hand on his shoulder for a brief moment before the massive man took his place beside him. Lyra phased through the main terminal, causing the AI to squeak and scramble aside.
“It’s worse than that,” she said grimly. “They’re already blaming Dregs for the structural damage and the crime spikes, not just in the Undercity, but all over Titan.”
He nodded grimly, the weight of all the decisions weighing on him. “Lyra, Korric. We are going to need enhancements and weapons. We need specialty-grade weapons and not a lot of time to get them.”
“I have already targeted a few areas and we have two old Tech Enforcers to help,” Korric said.
Vexal nodded. “Our next step will be proving we are a separate force, peaceful unless provoked. It is pivotal we use this time wisely. If we don’t—” He didn’t finish his sentence. He didn’t need to. They had no choice now but to stand their ground and fight back.


























Chapter 25
Rhea Vaelith was officially panicking. Commander Voxx was breathing down her neck, waiting for her report, the full force of the AAN’s top brass was waiting for results, and every monitor in the diagnostics bay was blinking red. No results, just failure on eerie readouts while the medtechs couldn’t even get proper scans, let alone pull viable metrics.
Nyx had collapsed from exhaustion in Sector Zero and had been brought in immediately. After they hooked her up to the system, her energy had triggered a major surge, a violent one that had nearly killed Rhea. She had never seen anything like it before, and the readouts were all in the negatives. As if Nyx’s body was draining energy from everything nearby. Power tools, the arrays, and even people. They had to isolate her before she did too much damage and the techs were literally leeched dry.
She had tried to call Darius twice, but both times he was still on patrol in the Undercity, caught cleaning up the fallout of the latest Dreg rampage. She glanced toward her private bay he usually docked at, her pulse racing, but her bay still stood empty.
She sprinted back to Nyx’s containment unit; it was humming unnaturally and every diagnostic line was a jagged branch of static. Another tech came running up, safety gear melted, his face an unhealthy shade of white.
“It’s getting worse!” he gasped. “I think we need to move—”
“We wait for Captain Valkyrie!” she snapped, her voice sharp from her rising stress levels. Her emerald skin flushed darker and her energy buzzed uncomfortably over old wounds. She forced herself to breathe; she needed to figure out a plan, and fast.
Suddenly, she felt a blazing pulse of heat behind her, the tech flinching away and heading back down to shed his safety gear and probably get checked out.
“Rhea! I’m here!” Darius stormed down the corridor, his uniform scorched and bloodied, acid still evident on his gauntlets. But his energy signature felt solid and stable, that was good at least.
“She’s not responding,” she said hurriedly as he stripped his gear. “She’s drawing energy from anything and everything, and I thought you might know something about this.”
“I can fix this,” he said shortly.
Rhea opened her mouth to argue, or at least get more details. But he was already moving into Nyx’s containment unit, heat visibly rolling off him. She reached for her console instinctively as he strode across the room without hesitation, dropping to his knees beside Nyx’s broken frame.
He touched Nyx gently and Rhea nearly screamed as she saw him unleash a blast of Magma energy directly into Nyx. He picked her up gently, cradling her with unusual tenderness, his brow furrowed as if he was bearing the weight of the whole world.
Rhea watched as the metrics lit up across every display and she rushed to her main console, her fingers flying. Her eyes widened as the numbers surged. Darius and Nyx’s resonance were synchronising. Rhea shook her head, trying to understand what she was seeing as Darius’s output held steady, feeling Nyx’s energy in real-time without being overdrawn. Nyx’s vitals jumped instantaneously and, from what Rhea could gather, her body was healing. Even Darius’s wounds began healing.
When it ended, Nyx slumped peacefully back onto the bed. Her body stabilised and it looked like she fell asleep instantly. Darius backed out carefully before walking out without a word.
Rhea grabbed him by the arm and pulled him into her office, immediately slapping a lock on the chamber door.
“What. Was. That?” she squealed as she pulled up Nyx’s room comms. She wanted to make sure nobody disturbed her newfound peace for the moment.
Darius ran his hand through his soot-streaked hair, looking renewed and fresh. “We had a moment during training,” he said slowly. “She told me… a little. But everything’s been moving so fast, we never got the chance to really… get into it.”


Rhea exhaled through her nose, biting her tongue because she wanted to yell at the giant man. He should have reported it immediately, reported it to her, but yelling was not going to help anything. “I’m building her energy profile,” she said instead, turning to her console. “Tracking her output from today, which started with your incident this morning. She has healed, warped, done who knows what, warped, and then the Sector Zero incident. She honestly shouldn’t even be alive, let alone synergising on this scale.”
Darius shifted uncomfortably. “She’s got support,” he muttered.
“No,” she corrected. “You are her support. And if you’re not there, if you can’t get there in time? What happens then?”
Darius didn’t answer. His molten eyes studied her carefully and she wished she knew what he was thinking. She stood up, frustrated, snapping a resonance band on his bicep, and she noted how it was almost twice the size of her head. “This will buy us some time,” she said, drawing blood steadily. “They’ll want samples and they’re going to want her in containment. But if we study you, her primary energy source, it seems, we can stall.”
Darius nodded, but clearly looked confused.
“You two make an incredible pair. The levels between the two of you are just unheard of, and I have never seen stabilisation like that. The AAN will be beyond hesitant to split you up. And this means she’s not a god; she has limits and is still very mortal.”
Darius nodded again, clearly thinking things over.
“She has got to stop vanishing, though,” Rhea continued, turning back to her desk. “The AAN has eyes and she’s a public figure now. Which means…”
She turned back to him. “If you were bonded, formally, things would change for the better and the normal.”


His eyes sharpened and he tilted his head.
“If you both bonded, you would be granted additional medical protection. For the most viable possible embryos, they would want you together and unstressed so… at the very least, retirement. Not even the High Chancellor dare raise such a stink at a time like this.”
Darius nodded slowly. “I’ll think about it.”
Rhea nodded with a sigh and released his arm from the resonance band. “You should head back here after your shift. She’ll probably need you and I might have news.”
He nodded and retrieved his gear before stepping toward the open balcony, stopping at the entrance of his transport. “Thank you, Rhea.” His deep voice rumbled across her office. And then he was gone.
She stood alone in her lab, her screens surrounding her in a comforting blue glow. She ran a hand across her eyes and spun toward her desk again. She had to write this report in a way that would not only let her study Nyx, but give Nyx and Darius a chance at something other than permanent containment.






















Chapter 26
Varian moved with a painful limp, his thighs and ribs still aching, slowly healing from Vexal’s Rot. He limped past rows of biofabricated monsters, hissing and growling softly in their tanks, pulsing with latent violence in their limbs. These weren’t Sweetlings anymore, not after what Fane had done to them.
His stomach churned as he felt Fane looming behind him, saying nothing, but watching his every move coldly. His presence pressed in like a weight against Varian’s spine, judging him. He hated the feeling of being watched by someone smarter, crueller, and infinitely more unpredictable than the monsters they’d grown.
He adjusted the final gene modulation, his fingers trembling slightly. The last batch was a set of true monstrosities, and Fane was ecstatic. These monsters couldn’t target precise locations; they were hardly even sentient. They went straight for the nearest and strongest power source, like feral, energy-starved beasts. Fane wanted to destroy as much of Titan as he could, in the shortest time possible.
When he was done, Varian stepped back and gave Fane a stiff nod. Fane said nothing and began checking over his work as if he were already bored. Varian waited until he was waved away to take his leave, trying not to show his limp as he exited the reactor and climbed into his personal transport.
As the craft lifted above the congested cities, the nausea returned in a rush. His mind spun and he resisted the urge to lie down. He had made a deal with Fane for science, power, and scientific breakthroughs people never dreamed were possible. But Fane was truly insane and a ticking time-bomb with no allegiance. Winning a war meant nothing if the whole world was destroyed in the process.
Varian landed with a soft click and he moved as quickly as his bruised frame could allow, hobbling into the crystal heart of the Ascension Council Chambers. The air was thick inside; gilded light refracted across the crystal like gold. At the far end of the chamber sat High Chancellor Mereen, her arms braced against her desk, a young Apex hopeful behind her synergising away quietly and obediently.
“Have you heard from the shadow kid?” she asked loudly, without a preamble.
Varian nodded and hobbled in closer, catching his breath. “Yes, ma’am, Bryn Alaric is in exceptional control of his mutation now. He’s run three deep in-field infiltrations already. You didn’t even see him at the Colosseum!”
Mereen’s thin brow arched; she smiled thinly, her opal-hued body moving back and forth rhythmically. “Perfect. Then it’s time to officially deploy him. The rebellion was an unpleasant surprise. We need to know everything about them. Locations, weapons caches, leadership, all of it. Down to the last sympathiser.”
Varian nodded. “It will be done immediately. About the Sweetlings… they…”
“Did far more damage than your reports had promised?” she snapped, cutting him off. “I know Fane tricked us. Tricked us all. Played at being weak so he could sneak around essentially free to do whatever he wished behind our backs. Now we’ve got a full-blown war and media disaster on our hands. Increase every mutation programme. I want spikes across all districts so we have enough people to even deal with this mess.”
Varian swallowed hard, slightly relieved he wasn’t being blamed for this. “Yes, ma’am.”
Mereen stood abruptly, waving the young Apex out of the room, her expression flat as she sat at her desk without even putting a robe on. “You know we are on a short, short clock,” she said, clicking through different screens. “Fane has proven his actions are only going to escalate, and we still haven’t secured 001X.”
Varian’s heart fluttered in his chest slightly. “Is the AAN stepping in?” he asked carefully.
“Negative. She doesn’t respond well at all to any kind of force,” Mereen said, shaking her head. “She is too reactionary, and right now I don’t have the manpower or resources on hand to properly handle a temper tantrum. No, I want illusion; I want a clean new fold, no cracks.”
He rubbed his jaw nervously. “We could have Fane approach her.”
Mereen looked up sharply. “Perfect.”
He resisted the urge to smile; Fane’s aggressive nature was sure to push the subject into paranoia. She would have to pick a side, and Mereen and the AAN were sure it would be theirs. They had no idea about Vexal’s ties to her. She was almost sure to go with Vexal, and when she did, he would be there in the wings, quietly waiting to step in.
“I will see to it as soon as she is back in the field?” he said with a question in his voice.
Mereen frowned, clearly weighing the pros and cons. He decided to give her a small push. “She will make for a good unifying figure with Valkyrie. And he will be sure to keep her in line and available.”
Mereen finally nodded, standing and waving a hand toward the door. “She’s going to change the course of Titan,” the Chancellor said as Varian saluted shortly and turned to leave. “And you are going to be there to make sure.”
Varian smiled to himself, the doors closing behind him in a clang of polished crystal, as he limped to his private chambers deep within the marrow of Helion’s Tree Spire, to his own private sanctum. As he drew closer, the corridors grew quieter and the lush super-light was replaced with the soft hums of sterile walls and pale lights. His office was vast, cold, and windowless. Rows of projections blinked in resonance-blue liquid, each humming a soft electronic lullaby.
He eased into his chair with a groan, the tension in his spine unrelenting. The last day still burned in his mind. Vexal’s bloodied body on top of his, violating him. Fane and Vexal’s energy signatures, overclocked and irreparably dangerous.
They were beautiful.
He felt tears run down his leathered cheeks at the thought. He hated how beautiful Fane’s work was. Brutal, grotesque, but layered with an elegance that made Varian feel like an amateur.
He rubbed his face and pulled up his display, going immediately to Myth’s feed. Or better yet, 001X’s feed. She was so visible now to the public, critical to their success. If the AAN wanted to control the future, they needed her loyalty, and if there was going to even be a future, then Fane couldn’t just be allowed to run rampant to destroy all of Titan. Rule it, yes; destroy it from the ground up, no.
Additionally, he knew it was only a matter of time before she started remembering things, especially after coming in direct contact with a Catalyst Well. She would at least remember him or Voxx.
He grinned wickedly from ear to ear, easing back in his seat, a quiet laugh escaping his throat. Of course, Thallion Voxx.
Perfectly placed, and with the subject's, no doubt altered, memories, he would be able to push her right into the AAN’s arms. With Valkyrie there to comfort her in a time of need, and her conclusion of her past, she would agree to let the AAN do everything they could to get her answers.
He pulled his console forward, fingers flicking rapidly through encrypted directories. Layers upon layers of sealed files rolled past his view. Decades of unauthorised data he had collected. His personal archive of brilliance.
Finally, he found what he was looking for. He had personally collected it decades of cycle-years ago during another terrible time when things had become too… difficult. Two things. The first one, a detailed log of the corrupted experimental trials with just enough intact data to paint a very specific picture. It told of two subjects, perfectly compatible with Quasar-tier energies, that were subjected to repetitive, extreme trauma exposure. After each event, they went through extensive memory corrective simulation trials. It was beautiful and detailed, one of his more prized sets of experiments, if he did say so himself.
And the second was only a file name and the access codes needed to read it without redaction. Time-stamped and untraceable, it was the day that Subject 001X was officially declared terminated.
Varian dropped the data as a packet deep into one of Voxx’s personal caches, one he knew the Commander obsessively monitored for leaks of unauthorised traffic. He would find it sooner rather than later.
And once he did, the Commander would inevitably leak it to Myth, either seeking to see if she remembered or if she had returned from the dead. He would have to kill her again!
A soft, giddy laugh bubbled from Varian’s throat; he knew the next step was clear. Fane’s monsters. Where to drop them? He hummed to himself as he pulled up the topographical map of Titan and tapped into the subterranean overlays. The central city was too predictable and had robust automatic protection, so that wouldn’t do. The Verge was unstable, but held too many allies. No, he needed… Skelter Reach.
Deep enough in Dreg territory that the damage wouldn't leak to any of the main cities, and close enough to a Catalyst Well to stir panic. Not close enough for it to be in any real danger, just enough to thin out the herd a bit.
He pinned three specific coordinates, all circling an old subterranean hub where several abandoned supply routes converged. Perfect for the narrative that these monsters were Dregs going out of control, though he didn’t know how well that story would hold once the news caught whiff of these new monsters; they probably would sniff out the difference eventually. Something about Fane’s new creations caused a worry in the back of his mind, something that wasn’t quite right, but he shrugged it away. That would be for Fane to deal with, considering he started this war.
He uploaded the coordinates to Fane’s private server, included a rough threat-potential remark, and washed his hands of it. He stood and limped across his office to his lab, past stasis rows and suspended vials, all the way until reaching a surgical cradle. He ran his hand tenderly along the sterile edge, mentally taking stock of its condition. It was a viable method for extracting high-energy genetic material. Blood and matter primarily, and not just a drop; he needed to gather enough litres, preferably with the subject in immediate distress. And he needed updated matter from both of his primary subjects. All he needed to do was to get it activated before Fane set his monsters loose. He worked quickly; the clock was ticking, and even though Fane wasn’t there, he felt him over his shoulder, surveying his work, there to punish and correct as needed. And Varian relished the feeling.
























Chapter 27
Myth sat up with a sharp breath, her whole body tense and sore. The ceiling above her was dim and slightly familiar, but it wasn’t the containment room. The warmth next to her grounded her, and she didn’t need to turn to know where she was. Darius, his presence like a low ember lying beside her and soothing her nerves. She let her hand drift over the sheets, and his eyes flew open the second she moved, his arm already coming around her waist, pulling her to him tightly. “How do you feel?” he asked, his voice raw with worry.
She gave him a small smile. “I feel… great?” She blinked again, her words sounding strange in her ears. “I mean, I don’t remember too much, honestly. But thank you for, well, everything.”
“Of course,” he murmured, his grip on her tightening just slightly. “But you’re only here on supervision. I told them I’d keep my eyes on you at all times. Figured you wouldn’t appreciate indefinite containment.”
She exhaled hard, relief rushing through her chest. “Thank you,” she whispered, gently pressing a kiss to his cheek.
He gave her a crooked grin, tiredness still clinging to his face. “Well, I can’t exactly do much without my partner.”
She giggled a bit, warmth spreading through her chest. She shifted, rolling on top of him and resting her head against his collarbone. She felt his body relax under hers and she took a deep breath, breathing in his smoky scent.
“So then what?” she asked.
He rubbed his hand over her back lazily. “We’ve got increased patrols after the monster incident. But you’re on light duty unless something crazy hits.”
She raised an eyebrow. “So what do I do then?”
“You're doing public image work. Direct orders from the High Chancellor herself.” He smirked proudly. “Smile, sign autographs, assure the masses that we have everything under control.”
She groaned dramatically. “Please, no…”
He laughed. “Don’t worry, I’ll do all the talking. Just don’t punch anyone important.”
“No promises,” she snorted.
He smiled gently at her, his eyes locking with hers. “Myth. We need to talk.”
Her smile faded as he shifted beneath her, gently brushing a stray lock of hair behind her ear.
“First, really. How do you feel?” he asked quietly.
She opened her mouth to reassure him, but she snapped it shut without a word, letting her body fall back onto his chest gently. She could hear his heartbeat, slow and strong, as her fingers traced the scars along his ribs. “Physically, I think I’m okay,” she said quietly. “Mentally and emotionally, not so much.”
His hand moved in soothing circles along her back, letting her take her time.
“My energy is still pretty low,” she admitted, chewing her lip. “And yesterday, I don’t know. I felt awful after I left. I went to see a friend, but it… it didn’t go great. And then my comtech went out, and by the time I got back…”
“You really scared me,” he said softly.
“I’m sorry.” She was slightly shocked by his vulnerability.
“I forgive you,” he said with a lopsided smile, pulling her tighter. He was quiet for a moment before clearing his throat. “While you were out of commission, they took a few blood samples from me. Tests and the usual.”
She tensed involuntarily.
“Next, they want to poke around in you for a bit,” he continued softly, as if sensing her apprehension. “But after that, if you get the okay, you will be cleared for duty.”
Her heart leapt slightly at the prospect of being out in the field again so quickly. But something still bothered her. “How many times did you have to…?” she asked hesitantly.
He blinked, confused for a second, before a look of clarity crossed his face. “Synergise with you?”
She nodded, her cheeks heating up.
“A few,” he said, his voice laced with tiredness. “I had to come in a few times between shifts. It was… a long day for me.”
She bit her lip as something twisted inside her chest. She saw the exhaustion creeping in at the corners of his eyes, his normal staunch bravado gone.
He did so much for me, she thought, he has always been there for me. And he has no one.
Her arms moved on their own as she wrapped them tighter around his neck, pressing her cheek to the warm line of his throat. He smelled like smoke and metal, and she felt a tear slip down her cheek before she could stop it.
“I’m so sorry, Darius,” she whispered.
“Hey now,” he murmured. “Look at me, will ya?”
He tilted her chin gently, thumb brushing under her eye, the heat of his hands quickly drying her face.
“I’ll be alright,” he said, a crooked smile on his lips. “I promise.”
She felt a strong ache in her heart; he had been there for her, kept her secrets, stood up for her repeatedly, she thought. She felt a strange warmth grow inside her, her energy rising beneath her skin, moving through her like a quiet, rushing current. She leaned in, gently landing a soft kiss on his lips. “Thank you,” she breathed, her voice trembling slightly.
Darius looked stunned, his molten eyes glowing a deep golden-orange underneath his dark lashes. “I promise I will always do everything in my power to keep you safe,” he murmured, his voice a soothing rumble, but his eyes bored into hers, a fierce intensity shining through the fatigue.
Despite his clear exhaustion, his body radiated an undeniable magnetism, pulling and teasing her energy. She let her fingers tangle in his hair, tugging lightly as she kissed him again, a fervour rising through her that set her nerves ablaze. A low groan escaped his lips as he pulled her closer, her chest arching into the hard planes of his body.
“Darius…” she gasped, her breath coming in shallow gasps as her body ached, her energy buzzing under her skin like a tidal wave.
He gripped her hips tightly, pulling her closer as he rolled them over, pinning her beneath his massive body. She felt her cheeks flush as she realised their mutual state of undress, her hair a dishevelled mess splayed across his pillows. His eyes burned into hers as he settled his weight between her thighs, the heat of his body searing her skin.
An intoxicating rush of adrenaline clouded her vision as he crushed his lips to hers in a fierce kiss. She felt herself meeting his passion without hesitation, gasping as his hand drifted up her thigh, his body pushing her legs further apart. He paused, watching her face intently, giving her a moment to settle before claiming her lips in another searing kiss, stealing her breath away.
His hips rolled against her, letting her feel the thick, hot ridge of his arousal, her own body reacting with a burning need for him. In that moment, she lost herself in the storm of her own need, feeling the slide of their sweat-slicked bodies as he moved gently, his body rocking hers with powerful thrusts. She felt Darius squeeze her knee and she breathed a sigh of relief, thankful for having seen enough news bulletins to come up with a diplomatic response. She admired Darius all the more for how easily he seemed to handle the questioning.
Abazara was rambling on while Nyx only half-listened, letting Darius wrap the interview with ease as the timer chimed and the ON AIR sign blinked off. As soon as the lights dimmed, she let out her breath and turned to stand as the assistants removed her mic. She watched as Darius said a few quiet words to Abazara before he turned and stood, the main doors opening and light flooding into the studio. He ushered Nyx in front of him, and they followed security out down a side exit, the roar of the press echoing from the main halls. As they entered Darius’s transport, she felt herself slightly relax, finally safe from hundreds of eyes that followed them everywhere. They had barely sat down when Darius’s comtech chirped alive. He tapped his wrist, and a cascade of glowing interfaces shimmered around his forearm. Without missing a beat, he started speaking in clipped tones, sending directives and, from what she could tell, scheduling missions. The entire cabin responded to his voice, the walls shifting to display tactical overlays as he issued a live debrief to the AAN core systems.
Myth turned, leaning her head against the glass as she watched Titan roll by beneath them. The sky was fractured into soft pinks and violets as twilight began to fall on the horizon, and she realised they had been out all day. Time had skipped past her; it felt like it was only a few hours ago they were having breakfast, but the emptiness in her stomach let her know that it had, in fact, been a full day.
When they landed, Darius leaned over, swiping away the many screens. “I’ve got an in-person report. You can come if you want, or you can wait for me in my room.”
She didn’t hesitate. “Room.” She had had enough of AAN bureaucracy for today.
He gave her a smile and leaned in, giving her a quick kiss on the head before he turned and left without another word. She watched him disappear before she walked into his suite, dropping onto his bed and letting her body fall back into the cool firmness.
Her mind raced with the events of the day as she released a long breath. This was the first time in the day that she had quiet-time to herself. She thought about visiting Vexal, but decided against it quickly. That was a bad idea right now, not until she was sure that she had time. She didn’t know when Darius was going to be back and knew she couldn’t afford for him to come back to her gone. She felt a tinge of guilt in her gut and she rolled over, agitated. She had come here for revenge, and now she was planning a family with one of the people she was supposed to be destroying. And the thing was, she didn’t want to destroy the Apex Corps anymore. Change it, yes, without a doubt. But there was no coming clean now. Darius would never forgive her if he learned the truth, and Vexal would probably kill her if she betrayed him.
She absent-mindedly flipped on his media console and started flipping through the channels, trying to ignore the flood of worry threatening to break her mind. She wanted a family; she hadn’t lied about that. She didn’t know exactly when she had started wanting that, but the feeling had settled deep in her bones. But what would he do when he found out? He certainly wouldn’t take it as easily as he had learning her name. She watched the animated characters dance over the screen, trying to distract herself, trying to find a way out without losing everything she had worked so hard for.
It felt like hours had passed before she heard the door finally hiss open again, Darius stepping through quietly, his soft footsteps sounding like mini-explosions in the silence.
“Still breathing?” he asked jokingly as he approached the bed.
Myth turned her head and gave him a bright smile. “And kicking!”
He chuckled. “C’mon. Rhea messaged. There’s a dinner party and we’re the main guests.”
She groaned dramatically. “I thought you were going to be gone forever!”
“Good,” he smirked. “Means you missed me.”
She stood up, stretching, brushing her hands through her hair on the way down. “I need to change. I can’t go in my uniform.”
He nodded. “Same. Might be nice not to wear gear to dinner.”
That got a laugh from her, and she followed him into the adjacent room where clothing options had already been laid out for them. She chose a sleek black dress with iridescent threads that shimmered blue at the plunging neckline. The dress swept the floor around her ankles, and the folds of the cloth were decorated with dark-blue gems. It wasn’t the flashiest option, but not the plainest one either. It fitted snugly around her waist, and the back was essentially non-existent, being held together with a bejewelled clasp at her neck. Darius chose a fitted, dark-blue coat with etched lapels; a subtle golden insignia was stitched into the hem. His trousers were black and velvet-styled, the sides lined with the same blue as his coat.
“You look beautiful,” he breathed, coming up behind her and wrapping his arms around her tightly.
She turned and smiled up at him. “You look mighty handsome yourself.”
He offered her his arm and led them back out and into the transport.
It was only a short distance to the terrace lounge that Rhea had picked. It was in the Helion Core Gardens, a bioluminescent sanctuary tucked away from the winds and prying eyes. A crystalline path wound through the garden toward the dining terrace. Light filters sparkled above the trees, making everything below look like it was dipped in a gentle dusk, while the soft sound of music flitted throughout. Myth savoured the warmth of Darius’s hand on the small of her back as they came into full view of the terrace. It was a floating pavilion with white and gold columns; the tables were made of obsidian, softly lit, and suspended over a lake of luminous petals, while the water below shifted in a kaleidoscope of ripples and koi. Eira was already there to greet them, her white, frosty sheer dress flowing off a gilded bench, and her hair gently fluttering around ice-styled flowers. Her pale skin shimmered with carefully placed gems that glittered brightly in the soft light. General King stood next to her in a crisp, dark-red suit, talking animatedly with a waiter, clearly agitated. His suit was encrusted with rubies and jaspers, and he wore no shirt underneath. Instead, his chest was adorned with a golden tattoo that disappeared around his neck. He was motioning with a heavily ringed hand to platters of steaming delicacies and spiral glass decanters that were being placed by silver-plated drones.
As Myth and Darius approached, Rhea came into view, rising gracefully from her seat in a shimmer of dark green and living vines and flowers that curled around her skin.
“Finally!” Rhea said, giving Myth a gentle squeeze with her outstretched hand. “We’ve already ordered.”
“I don’t care if the chef’s credentials were reviewed by the Solar Chancellor himself,” King was saying smoothly, swirling a thin flute of wine between two fingers. “If the reduction separates in the atmosphere, it is, by definition, not a reduction.”
The waiter bowed and mumbled a jumble of words before speeding away.
Eira didn’t look at him as she plucked a candied seed pod delicately off a tray. “You’re going to give that poor man a nervous collapse.”
King exhaled dramatically, turning with a flourish. “Good. Let him fall into a coma and dream of proper technique.”
Rhea turned from Myth with a smile. “You know, not everyone was raised with crystal stemware and neural sommeliers.”
“Nyx, Darius. Perfect timing,” Eira greeted, turning slightly in a flurry of soft snow. “The General was just about to declare war on the sommelier-bot over the ’87 vintage Cygnus X-1.”
“It's about standards!” King exclaimed, the ruby clasps on his suit catching the light brilliantly.
Darius pulled out a seat next to Rhea for Myth, settling her gently before taking a seat on the other side of her. Before Myth could get comfortable, Commander Voxx arrived, dressed in a smooth, drape-styled onyx suit that subtly concealed his wings underneath. Something about his look caused Myth’s thoughts to stutter, something familiar. Lynx Kairen, who was dressed in steel-grey robes with heavy silver tassels and chains that swung around his waist and neck, flanked him.
“Gods, King,” Voxx drawled, “you would serve fourteen-hundred-credit vintages to distract from a slightly too-cool roux.”
The General laughed, his voice booming in the cosy space, clasping Voxx on the shoulder. “That’s assuming the wine wasn’t the plan all along. Besides,” his eyes swept the table, landing on her, “we have a new Quasar to celebrate.”
Voxx’s gaze followed King’s; his piercing platinum eyes felt like a knife through Myth’s heart. She smiled, trying to ignore her mind screaming, trying to ignore the memory of perfume orchids and the same voice, younger but just as deep, murmuring low against her ear. Her hand on his jaw as he whispered to her.
She felt Darius’s hand on her knee, bringing her back to the present, to Eira saying, “…which I’m sure she finds absolutely ghastly.” Her icy hand waved through the air in a flurry of snow.
“Well, the Solaris-Vaelith pact is already being challenged again,” Darius quipped. “They wasted no time.”
“The House of Cygnus thinks they can offer Rhea a better genetic pairing,” Eira huffed. “Can you imagine? Them? Their bloodline is practically diluted to artisan-grade.”
Rhea’s laugh held a lilt. “Lord Cygnus may be ambitious, but it seems fate is against him. I heard there were no mutations at all in his last birthing cycle.”
“He’s hoping to breed his way out of mediocrity, even approaching the House of Kairen,” Lynx snorted, undoing his coat and draping his athletic figure over a chair. Voxx seated himself next to him in a flourish, and the table was complete.
“Ambition without substance is just noise,” Voxx said, already reaching for his wine glass.
King nodded, turning to Myth. “And you, Nyx? Are you ready for the scandalous Null-lineages to hound you the way they hound our dear Eira?”
“There will be no hounding,” Darius interjected. “They hound Eira because she teases them.”
“I can’t help but toy.” Eira batted her eyes before laughing. “Besides, it’s not my fault they don’t know it's hopeless.”
The conversation flowed easily among them after that. Lynx lamenting that the betting pools were outrageous this Apex Crucible cycle, Rhea gossiping about a minor Apex lord caught synergising with a Null, and King regaling them with his and Eira’s monster takedown. She was laughing, enjoying the sweet and savoury taste of the food that she was served. Suddenly, as Voxx’s voice echoed across the table, she felt a stab of pain lance through her head.


“Come on, Thallion!” She was calling to a young Voxx. Pale and serious, poised with his hands clasped behind his back, six strong blades of light shimmered around him.


In a flash, the memory was gone, leaving a cold feeling to rush over her. She downed her drink and blinked hard, trying to focus on the conversation.
“Instability creates opportunity,” Voxx was stating. “Hysteria is a resource in small amounts. High Chancellor Mereen is already spinning the monster attacks into new recruitment drives.”
“All that means is that the main houses need to strengthen their alliances. Get off their laurels and actually work to retain their statuses,” Rhea added, rising from her seat and walking to the bar at the edge of the pavilion. She retrieved a vivid green drink before turning with a flourish. “Nulls, in general, need to finally merge into Dregs.”
The table murmured in agreement, and Myth nodded along, her head still aching.
The conversation swirled distantly around her as her drink settled into her bones, the warm fire of something alcoholic burning away at her nerves. The wind tickled her skin, cool and refreshing, and she stood gently, walking to the edge of the pavilion, looking over the lower gardens and elite crystal hubs below.
She felt another lance through her skull, the conversation fading in and out in a roar.
“The press is calling them ‘Fane’s Horrors’. It's so melodramatic, but it does sound good for an Apex-level threat.” Darius's voice cut through the fog; he had come up behind her. “Nyx?” His voice was low in her ear, his breath warm against the chill. “You good?”
She blinked hard, looking up at him. She nodded and gave him a warm smile. “I just needed to stretch my legs,” she said easily. He nodded, turning back to the conversation, but not leaving her side.
She blinked again, and suddenly she was back in the cold dark. The hard walls of the training room closed in around her as the sharp, stinging pain of energy suppressors flooded the air. Thallion stood over her, huffing in exertion, his face a web of disappointment and pain. A sharp voice echoed above them: “Again. The goal is to reach a baseline.” The voice echoed with a chilling sense of familiarity.
“Myth,” Darius whispered in her ear. She whirled to face him, crashing back to the present and almost knocking his drink out of his hand.
“I’m sorry. I must have drunk too much,” she murmured. Darius nodded and wrapped a warm arm around her shoulders comfortingly, turning her back to his chest. She leaned against him and observed that she at least wasn’t the only one tipsy. King and Eira were swept up in their own private conversation, passionately debating each other, their drinks sloshing in their hands with their enthusiastic flourishes. They made their way to the lower level of the pavilion, a low-hanging tree slightly obscuring them as they made their way into the lower gardens. Rhea and Lynx seemed to also be locked in conversation, but of a more intimate sort. They were wrapped in a gentle green mist, and eventually Lynx stood, swaying lightly, before pulling Rhea up with him. Voxx came up behind her and whispered something that made the emerald woman giggle before all three of them disappeared down a dimly lit path deep into the cover of the trees.
Myth turned back towards Darius, swaying slightly, the drink making her eyes fuzzy as she looked up at him. He was watching her intently with a small smile on his lips.
“Walk with me?” he asked, offering his arm to her.
She took it gratefully, leaning into his steady warmth as he led her out into the endless expanse of lush beauty. The walkways floated slightly off the ground, edged with giant glowing pearls that shimmered in the water. Pink petals drifted endlessly down, perfuming the air with a sweet scent and dropping gently on the flowing streams.
They walked slowly in the quiet, her head on Darius’s bicep as they walked.
“You lost in thought again?” Darius asked in a murmur, slowing slightly to turn and look down at her.
Myth’s throat went dry, the drink buzzing in her head making her slow to formulate a sensible answer. “I hadn’t expected such a nice get-together.”
He didn’t speak, and Myth kept her gaze forward. “Plus, who would have thought the people who could crack the planet in half are worried about soup temperatures?”
He gave a soft laugh, turning and coming to a gentle stop. “Yeah,” he said. “I forget sometimes that not everyone grew up with this.”
She looked at him finally. “Did you?”
“Mostly,” he said, brushing a strand of hair out of her face. “The Apex schools, elite combat forces with formal table etiquette by thirteen. I knew a salad fork before I knew a friend.”
Myth nodded, resisting the urge to chew on her lip. He brought her hand to his lips and kissed her knuckles lightly, leaving a blazing trail of fire and nerves in his wake. He pulled her into his chest, his lips brushing her ear, sending shivers down her spine. “Let’s go home.”




















Chapter 28
The room was quiet save for the sound of the energy suppressors, but Thal’s internal clock told him it was at least close to oh-four-hundred hours. There was minimal light coming from the embedded floor strips and the room still held the warmth of energy.
He blinked hard, his head aching from the alcohol, but his body felt refreshed. Rhea shifted behind him, her soft fingers grazing the side of his thigh in passing. The door slid shut silently behind her green frame, and he rolled over to watch Lynx, who was still asleep. His spine curved perfectly into the silk sheets, his arm dangling over his face carelessly. Thal moved gently, untangling his legs from Lynx and the sheets, and padded across the room to his desk, not bothering to get dressed yet. His wings sagged limply behind him, when they should have been bright and alert. He sighed, leaning back gingerly in his chair, watching Lynx move softly, his breathing shifting into a soft snore.


He opened his terminal, the bright white and cold against his bare chest. He checked all the standard systems without a hitch before going deeper into the AAN processors, looking. Something was wrong, and he didn’t know what exactly. Myth’s presence, yes, but something more than that bothered him. She had looked directly at him, the same way she used to, just for a split second.
He shook his head. The terminal blinked a silent red light; the system must have flagged something. He sifted through the data until he found the problem: a packet lodged in a black-blind cache, zero trace of where it came from or where it was intended to go.
He flipped it open, his fingers flying along the keyboard working on the encryption. It wasn’t the average sequencing either, at least a Tier Eight. He was rewarded when the file barfed its contents:


SUBJECT: 001X // “MYTH” TERMINATION RECORD (corrupted download)
TRIAL 731-J: RESONANCE LOG (Thallion V. / Myth A.) (partial download)
 PROJECT CHIMERA
— PERSONNEL FILE: FANE S. DRYST 197F278 (terminated) 


He stared at the screen, the cursor blinking in steady intervals. A quiet pressure was building behind his sternum, a panic that made the air feel thin.
Behind him, there was a soft shift of silk and Lynx stopped snoring, sitting up and rubbing his eyes as he squinted into the dim light. “Thal?” His voice was thick with tiredness. “What time is it?”
“Around oh-four,” he answered quietly, his wings twitching weakly as he tried to discreetly catch his breath.
Lynx rose from the bed, stretching languidly before he moved soundlessly across the floor, the air cooling subtly around him as his Nullify energy regulated slowly.
“Your wings,” Lynx said quietly, his face tight.
“I’m fine,” he said flatly.
“Liar.”
He exhaled through his teeth, and before he could move, Lynx had grabbed the terminal, turning it around, his eyes quickly surveying the information. Thal leaned back gingerly, refusing to look at Lynx’s face.
“What the hell is this?” Lynx asked finally.
He didn’t answer right away, instead looking at the highlighted text Lynx shoved under his nose.
“You’re on this file, Thal,” Lynx pressed. “Why are you on this file with Fane Dryst?”
“I don’t know. I haven’t read it yet,” he finally answered, his voice low. “I was running security checks. It was buried in a blind cache.”
Lynx leaned in closer, his usual cool demeanour replaced by a sharp intensity, his silver-grey eyes piercing into Thal’s soul before he turned back to the screens. His fingers tapped through the terminal, and Thal leaned in to see him click through the fragmented entries.
“For gods' sake, Thal…” Lynx’s voice came out barely above a whisper. He began to read aloud, his voice shaky. “Resonance Trial 731-J, Subject Thallion V. Wing integrity at thirty-eight percent capacity. Chronic degeneration… Subject Myth A? Bio-restorative output over ninety-two percent post…”
Thal felt a sharp pang in his chest as a familiar ache shot through the joints of his wings, a pain he lived with every single day, feeling amplified under the stress.
Lynx’s eyes flicked to Thal’s and then back to the screen. “Forced synergy… forced synergy? Thal!”
He leaned back, squeezing his eyes shut as a memory fragment pierced through his mind like a shard of broken glass. A blinding, warm, and violet light, and Myth’s dark face, strained and streaked with tears. He felt waves of guilt wash over him, and he bit back tears until his mouth tasted like iron.
“Forced synergy protocol, activated,” Lynx continued, his voice shaky. “Subject Myth’s output is… the data is corrupted… causing unprecedented stabilisation in Subject Thallion’s wing matrix. Cellular regeneration recorded at four hundred… four hundred and fifty percent above baseline!”
He could feel Lynx’s eyes on him again, and he squeezed his eyes shut and turned away. He didn’t need to be scolded right now; he needed to figure out a plan. Lynx grabbed him roughly by the jaw, forcing him to face him, his face full of fury. “Thal… you… this girl? Your wings? You need to explain to me what is going on. Now.”
Thal pulled his jaw away roughly and clicked to the next file, shoving the screen in Lynx’s face before standing and walking to the large bay window behind them.
“Termination record?” Lynx’s face looked sick in the reflection of the window. “Incident 731 resulted in an uncontrolled resonance cascade. Containment Breach Sector 4… the data’s corrupted. Fane Solomon Dryst reported on-site, causing catastrophic failure.”
A cold wave washed over Thal, and the panic he had felt earlier set in now with a cold, crushing weight. He knew Fane had been there; even if he couldn’t trust his memories, he knew Fane was the cause of this.
“Subject Thallion V. experienced critical exposure, recommended for immediate Level Five memory alteration protocol…” Lynx’s voice died away. “They wiped your memory?” His face was a mask of horror, his jaw slack.
“Read the rest,” Thal commanded, his voice a low growl.
Lynx’s gaze dropped back to the screen, his voice shaky. “Subject Myth A. deemed too unstable a variable, the data’s corrupted here… recommendation: permanent termination.”
The words hung in the air, the silence pressing in on his ears. Permanent termination. Who gave this report? What happened to Dryst? This left more questions than answers, but at least he knew for certain: he shouldn’t trust his memories about anything. Even the pleasant ones.
His wings flared involuntarily, a sharp, grinding pain tearing through him. He staggered, his knee hitting the cold floor, his wings dropping against the tile. Lynx was at his side instantly, his Nullify energy surging through Thal's body, dampening the spike.
“Thal,” Lynx started. But he stopped and shook his head. “Does synergising with Rhea still help?”
Thal gave a stiff nod, his jaw tight. Only a select few knew about his condition, and those few were chosen to help, not to be a burden. He needed to get Myth back; the others could only temporarily slow the degeneration. Even Rhea’s healing energy couldn’t undo even a little degeneration without almost dying. He straightened up, standing tall, the pain in his back adding to his anger. Fane had lied, no surprise. The AAN had lied, no surprise. But Myth was lying too, and he had to find out how deep her lies went. She certainly didn’t remember much, or she would have gone berserk already—
“Who is Myth?” Lynx asked, breaking into his train of thought. “And don’t lie to me. I know you don’t remember everything, but it's clear you know who she was. So who was she?”
He didn’t answer right away as he pressed the heel of his hand into his temple, forcing back the throb that was building behind his eyes. “She was someone who could heal me. We used to be… close,” he said finally, his voice tight.
Lynx stood still behind him, his Nullify-field still tight, pressing against his body defensively. “So what do we do?”
He turned from the window finally, narrowing his eyes. “We… we do nothing. It’s just some old cache floating in the void.”
“But—”
Thal raised a hand. “This is old information. Myth is dead. Clearly. Besides emotional drudgery, there is nothing more to say.”
“Fane—”
“Fane has always been and always will be a problem unless we kill him. Get dressed; we have work to do,” he said resolutely.
Lynx clearly realised there was no room for argument and his Nullify energy subsided. He turned slowly and gathered his robes from the night before. Thal watched him in silence, his mind buzzing. Lynx gave him one last look before he walked out, the door hissing closed, finally leaving Thal in silence.
He knew exactly what he needed to do as he moved back to the console, his fingers moving swiftly over the terminal as he sat down. Within seconds, he’d pulled up Darius’s schedule: he was set to do another public viewing and on light-duty protocol. Standard Apex procedure. He tapped around quickly: at midday, a Chancellor’s check-in, originally scheduled for the next week. Thal entered his credentials and closed the tab grimly. He then opened the secure communication line to Mereen and typed using sparse, coded words. She needed to hold Valkyrie there, with no optics, so that fiery bastard wouldn't know until it was too late. They all wouldn’t know until it was too late, all except Varian. He pulled up the secure relay channel and typed rapidly:


1105–1115 HRS. GARDEN PROMENADE LOOP 3: DEPLOY 1 SWEETLING


Thal felt a chill down his spine as he pressed send; he remembered them clearly. Not fatal to Apexes, but definitely would do its job of causing mass panic. He closed the terminal, rolling his neck slowly. He stood, stretching his arms above his head, relishing the pain in his back when he heard his door hiss open again. Lynx was back, dressed in his normal black uniform, his grey eyes a sharp contrast to his slate-black hair. Thal admired his olive-brown skin, holding a hand in Lynx’s direction. Lynx walked slowly across the room, his Nullify-field brushing gently against Thal’s skin as he approached.
“You good?” Thal asked, capturing his chin in his hand gently.
“Yeah.” Lynx exhaled slowly, looking at him searchingly before closing the distance between them. Thal pulled him over to the bed, tugging Lynx’s clothes back off.
Until he found a way to permanently fix his wing degradation, synergy was the only thing holding them together. Synergising with Lynx was sharp, clean, precise, and perfect. His body responded to his energy immediately, and the weight of his wings lifted.


After he found a permanent fix to his degradation problem, he would be with Lynx for good, not just to save himself.
























Chapter 29
Fane watched his monsters breathing in the dark. The only light was coming from the holding chambers, just enough to show their terrible countenance. He felt a smile stretch over his face as he examined his creations. Row upon row of ever-mutating amalgams of bone, flesh, and steel, growing and filling their containment tanks.
Varian stood with his hands folded neatly, his red, beady eyes wide with admiration, his excitement radiating off him.
“They are beautiful tonight,” Varian whispered, stepping reverently closer to the tank.
“They are starving,” Fane growled, his voice filtering through the exo-suit in a metallic rasp. He stood, a colossus of pulsating, corrupted tech well over ten feet tall, but the horrors in front of him dwarfed him. Forty years of obsessive genius poured into each mammoth frame. Their forms, well over twenty-five feet even when hunched over, stood as a perfect mockery of life as they hung suspended in the nutrient-rich fluid. Their massive, elongated arms ended in claws of sharpened Inconel, their heads covered in massive helmets of tool-steel, maws lined with row upon row of titanium teeth. Their ribs pulsed with slow, powerful energy, constantly generating and growing, their bodies in a never-ending, agonising state of flux.
“They are starving for blood. Violence. Fear,” he snarled, turning from the tanks. Varian’s red-lensed glasses were fogged with his heavy breathing as he took in the sight around him.
“They’re magnificent,” the scientist almost wept. “And perfect timing, too. We have received a directive… from Commander Voxx.”
Fane’s back stiffened, but he said nothing, taking the datapad from Varian, quickly reading the screen:


1105–1115 HRS. GARDEN PROMENADE LOOP 3: DEPLOY 1 SWEETLING


The datapad crumbled in his hand, the acid pouring from his fingertips, his eyes clouded over momentarily. The respirator hissed laboriously, trying to keep up with the strain. “Voxx,” he spat, the name tasting like bile in his mouth. The sound that ripped from his throat made Varian flinch, his teal gloves flying to his glasses.
“Deploy?!” he hissed. “A ‘Sweetling’?! He treats me like a common errand boy!” His fist slammed into the adjacent wall, shattering the reinforced metal, sending a shower of sparks into the dark. He stormed over to the console, his fingers hammering into the keys. “He wants the Garden Promenade. A park. Easily contained and sterilised.” A map of Titan flashed on the screen, landing on a central, glittering tower. “The Helix Spire in Aetherion,” he smiled.
Varian gasped with glee behind him. “Master! That is wonderful! The command pinnacle, the chapel, the Obris Vault!”
“Yours for the scavenging,” he said coldly.
“A Quasar-level battle there will surely be catastrophic. Their communications will be on the fritz!” The cherry-rat was almost jumping with joy.
The massive vat doors began to grind open, the grates beneath draining the containment tanks. The doors unfolded and, with the sound of tearing flesh, two of the monsters leapt out with terrifying speed. Their claws gashed the chamber floor as they let out a synchronised, ground-shaking roar that was part mechanic and part roar.
Varian was thrown back, his whole body trembling as he scrambled to his feet, laughing maniacally. “Oh, they are alive and kicking!” he giggled in pure ecstasy.
“Oh, they are much more than that, Varian,” Fane rumbled, his chest filling with paternal pride. He stepped forward, his exo-suit a mere toy next to the masterpieces he had wrought. He placed a gauntleted hand on the head of the nearest creature. “You see, your ‘Sweetlings’ are failures. Mindless, screaming, pathetic beasts. These…” He turned the creature's head to Varian. “…can think. They can reason. They can fight. And they can hate.”
Varian whipped his glasses off. “Think? But… but… the mutation process… the neural decay—”
“Is a problem only for lesser minds,” Fane snapped. “These were my most loyal servants, Varian. Dregs who worshipped me, who gave me everything. And in return,” he turned, showing a matching patch of skin and sinew, “I harvested them. I stripped them of their weakness, their fear, their pathetic, breakable bodies. I stitched their acid-resistant hides into my armour and gave them the strength of thousands of men. In perfect, indestructible, eternal vessels.”
The monster under his hand turned, its massive steel helmet retracted, revealing a singular glowing green optic in a mass of regenerative flesh and brain tissue. “Master,” it rasped.
“I want to rip Titan apart,” Fane continued. “It’s not just their flesh I want… it’s their souls. And I’ll start with the eyes.”
He slammed a fist on the activation panel, and the massive transport bay doors began to open to reveal a colossal funeral transport. He typed in the coordinates quickly, feeling his blood slowly simmer down, the joy of the moment finally settling over him like a cloak. “This is revenge, Varian. They thought they could cripple me, keep me from my rightful place. I am owed this! It is my birthright! It is my destiny! And NO ONE WILL GET. IN. MY. WAY!”
The monsters in front of him quivered in fear, and Varian’s face was a sickly-pale colour. He raised an arm to the transport and the two monsters were in it in a flash, their massive bodies barely contained, the thrusters groaning.
“Find me the strongest person. And kill them. KILL THEM ALL!” He punched in the release protocols, the transport pod sealing shut. The next second, they were on their way, moving toward Aetherion like a rocket.
Fane turned, the gears in his chestplate whining softly. “And now,” he said, his voice composed again, “I need to take care of my little uprising problem.”
Varian blinked out of his fevered awe, wiping the sweat from his forehead with a trembling hand. “You mean the Ash Blight Rebellion?” he asked tentatively.


Fane snarled, his hands already moving across the console, pulling up the network full of their rebel chatter. They thought they were being clever with their fracture-code pulses, encrypted signal trails, and botched attempts at radio silence.
“They’re festering in the Verge,” he muttered finally. “He wants me to come; he wants a spectacle. He always did like the theatre.” He pointed to the console, turning the screen towards Varian. “There, a convoy. More than likely a mobile clinic or lab filled with the sick and injured,” he sneered.
“Perfect!” Varian’s voice broke through in reverence. “But… it could be a trap, Master? They have kept well hidden until now—”
Fane’s laugh cut him off, the sound crackling through the exo-suit's modulator like static. “Pray tell, Doctor. What could they possibly trap me with? Vexal knows what I am capable of. He wouldn’t dare bait me.”
He brought up the payload interfaces; the convoy feeds flickered in the low-energy light, displaying row upon row of injured Dregs, no doubt the Dregs caught in Varian’s little Sweetlings.
“Bring me Specimen 88 and 97,” he ordered.
Varian darted off, issuing the release sequences to two more of Fane’s monsters. Acid sluiced into the drains as the vat doors peeled open, the beasts leaping forward towards Fane. He turned and stalked to the hangar roof, his exo-suit humming with the gathered energy. He turned, eyes raking over his creations one more time before he rocketed out into the early morning light.
In a flash the Verge sprawled beneath him, the gnarled tangles of green-and-black canopied over the dense forest in a protective shield. He slowed and spread his arms wide, lowering as acid spewed from his body in torrents. Acid that he had been storing for over twenty cycle-years, concentrated and vile.
The trees sizzled below him, the deluge boiling through the emerald canopy like fire. The red-and-green medical transport below them exploded in a halo of light. Screams echoed through his ears pleasantly as he swept low, enjoying the delightful chorus of pain. Behind him, he heard his monsters land like meteors, crashing through the burnt canopy and immediately laying waste to all they saw.
As Fane slowly descended, dust and blood swirled around his feet, misting with the acid and rising around his knees. He knew he didn’t hit the main transport carrying the civilians, but it was here, and something that large couldn’t move very fast in the tangled mess of a jungle. He would have Vexal’s little rebellion squashed before lunch. And get a bit of exercise while he was at it.


















Chapter 30
Mereen adjusted the delicate fall of her stark white gown, the medical-grade weave falling gently over her backless frame. She sat alone beneath the glowing dome of the Council Chamber, her eyes closed, the threads of thought whispering through the air like unseen silk. The massive taffeite table was currently empty save for her, the painite doors shielding her mind from the onslaught of thought.
Almost as if on cue, the massive red gemstone door swung open silently. Mereen opened her eyes to see the first to enter was Lord Cassien of the House of King, his polished gold breastplate casting fractured light against the floor. His posture and frown oozed with disdain, his thoughts loud and predictable: Dreg eradication, a pure upper class of Apexes and Luminals only.
Behind him, Lady Isalya Solaris drifted in like a winter mist, her light-blue skin tinged with frost, her voice high with disappointment. The House of Solaris were masters at intelligence gathering, medical research, and genetic preservation. Overseeing most of Cryothorne's facilities, they were essential to the balance of Titan. And Mereen knew, without having to use her psionic energies, that the Solaris family wanted Fane dealt with swiftly, wanting to avoid war at all costs.
General Titus Valkyrie strode in next, cloaked in burnished red, his fiery hair flaming with every word. His family kept Forgedeep’s military and industrial complex burning at full tilt, and had been for most of Titan’s history. After his daughter bonded with an Ashdog, he had taken in the mutt, seeing the usefulness in a mixed breed, she supposed. He was a weapon, though, and Mereen needed an army as it was. Valkyrie's anger was explosive; the rebellion had hit him the hardest, stealing military-grade weapons and tech, leaving the Luminals and Echelons scrambling to protect themselves.
He was in a heated conversation with the Ambassador for the House of Kairen. They had full control of the Nocturne district and a large hand in supplying enforcers for the T.E.B. Never appearing in person, they always sent an Ambassador through whom they spoke using neural links. The Ambassador was draped in opaque fabrics to shield his sensitive, pale skin from the UV rays, their nocturnal state clashing with the daylight hours.
Behind them trickled in minor envoys, some from the House of Vaelith to represent Blackroot Verge. The Verge having no formal house, but instead a commune of matriarchal-led houses. Their green skin stood in sharp contrast to their ivory robes, their voices melodious but grave.
The table was full, and the unease was thick enough for her to taste. “The quorum is full,” she said softly. “Let us begin.”
Cassien King was on his feet before she even finished speaking. “We should glass the Verge. Fane will burn out along with this pathetic rebellion.” He pounded the table with a thick finger. “We send the T.E.B. to clear out Sector Seven and then torch the place.”


Mereen wasn’t fazed; she had already begun latching onto the edges of his mind, his outburst a perfect distraction for her psionic energy. “Councillor King,” she said smoothly, “torching a whole district to kill one man is a strategy best left to madmen.”
Cassien sat down heavily, his lips twitching, but clearly lacking a formal argument against cold logic.
“We need precision.” Solaris stood delicately. “Fane is a cold wound, and we need to permanently end him, but not at the expense of millions.”
Titus Valkyrie snorted, standing in a fiery rush. “Precision? While my facilities are being stripped bare? Five caravans were gutted just this week! My own engineers are defecting to the uprising, and yet you think our biggest problem is the one-man show?”
The Nocturne Ambassador rose abruptly, turning his heavily veiled head toward Mereen and waiting patiently for Valkyrie to reseat himself. “Nocturne has no desire for a civil war, but if your Apex Corps cannot secure Fane soon, the full T.E.B. will be sent in. The populace does not take well to a military-controlled state, and we would be at an even greater risk of losing our control to the Rebellion. We would not only lose our places, but we would be outcast to the wilds without fail. If the people are merciful. And we know they will not be.”
A ripple of cold murmurs echoed through the room as he sat back down, his body clearly exhausted from the strain of the neural link over such a long distance.
Mereen's fingers traced a slow circle against the cold purple stone of the table; the mental threads in the room leaped out at her. Rage, fear, uncertainty. She stood quietly, walking around the table slowly. “We are being pulled into a war, not by the Rebellion's ideals, nor by Fane’s insanity,” she said, her bare feet whispering against the crystal floor. “We are being pulled in by fear. We cannot afford fear.”
“Then what,” Cassien muttered bitterly, “do you propose we do?”
Mereen stopped beside him, feeling her energy float around him like a halo. “We pull the rebellion apart at the seams. We infiltrate them quietly, and divide them into fractions. The Apex Corps will handle Fane in public acts of heroism. If the public wants to feel safe and protected, a crumbling rebellion will be the last place they would run. And,” she moved behind Lady Solaris, “I already have someone installed. They will be initiating contact today. Once they are trusted by them, it will only be a matter of time before the Ash Blight Rebellion falls apart.”
Having Bryn Aleric already in place was a balm the Council needed, and she let her psionic threads retract from their minds.
“Your confidence is noted, Chancellor,” Solaris said, standing, her frosty blue skin almost glittering under the light. “But this ignores the immediate, physical threat. The attack on the Catalyst Wells shows just how far Fane will go to destroy Titan.”
“The tremors from the Well Three attack caused massive magma displacement,” An envoy from the Verge said, standing briefly. “We lost two mining platforms and a full foundry. And they aren’t stopping!” 
The table erupted into heated conversation, and Mereen didn’t try to calm them. She drifted slowly to the head of the table, letting her psionic subjugation work slowly, brushing along the minds of the attendees. She tasted guilt on one envoy’s mind and looked deeper, finding thoughts of leaked routes, secret dossiers on sympathisers, and even thoughts of defecting.
It took a small effort not to react, and she made a mental note to plug the leak before too much damage was done.
“If Forgedeep falls, then we lose access to our main atmospheric stabiliser!” Valkyrie’s explosive flames flared over the table. “If the Dregs have their way, we will all be breathing poison within half a cycle-year!”
“Dryst is testing us!” Cassien shouted, his Solar Resonance energy shining brightly. “I move we take Dryst out immediately, especially if we are infiltrating the rebellion. Giving Fane any time will only result in havoc!”
An aide glided in through the doors softly, her face pale as a ghost. She almost ran to Mereen’s side, placing a datapad before her. Her mind flared reading the alert:


SOS: MIRIDAN HOLLOW ATTACKED: MEDICAL CONVOY TRAVELLING THROUGH THE VERGE TARGETED. REPORTS OF ACID RAIN. BIOCHEMICAL SIGNATURES MATCH FANE S. DRYST. SEND ALL REINFORCEMENTS AND THE T.E.B. ENFORCERS!


Mereen carefully placed the pad down, her hand shaking slightly. The room had gone stone-quiet, waiting for her next words. “Lord Cassien, General Valkyrie. Motion granted. Fane has attacked Miridan Hollow, Blackroot Verge's medical convoy. They are requesting not only immediate backup, but T.E.B. enforcers. I’ll be deploying Operatives King and Solaris to personally handle this threat.”
Cassien rose, his golden armour gleaming. “Perfect! I am satisfied,” he said staunchly. Valkyrie was already excusing himself, tapping into his comtech and barking orders, fire blooming from his irises.
Lady Solaris brushed her fingers through the top of her comtech, eyes scanning the details of the alert. “Very well. I will coordinate Cryothorne's aerial transports. We’ll bring medical and recovery drones.”
Mereen inclined her head. “Coordinate with the Vaelith family as well.”
Solaris nodded and turned, leaving a frosty cloud in her wake. The Nocturne Ambassador dipped his head slightly and followed Solaris out, the envoys hot on his heels. Cassien swept out with Valkyrie, already coordinating their weapons effort.
The painite doors closed behind them with a heavy thud, and Mereen closed her eyes for a beat. Fane’s unexpected attack being almost so close in time to Voxx’s scheduled one seemed like a directly malicious attack on their agreement. The rebellion was another unpleasant surprise, and she wondered if he was attacking Titan or lashing out against the rebellion specifically. With that monster, both were equally as probable.
She opened her eyes and checked the time, before she strode out of the Council Chamber to her office, waving away the hovering aides. She sat stiffly and pressed a chime, the doors swinging open once more to reveal Captain Darius Valkyrie. She could feel the warm thrum of his anxiety and welcomed him in with a wave of her hand. His black Apex uniform was immaculate, but his hair was tousled; she wondered if it was from him or someone else.
“Chancellor,” he greeted with a stiff salute.
“Captain,” she said smoothly, motioning for him to sit. He sat slowly, his posture rigid. He gave a glowing report on Nyx’s recovery, reintegration, and stability. She only half-listened, not even hiding her psionic probes, ensuring he was telling the truth.
“Chancellor, I would like to make an official request to permanently Bond with Operative Nyx. And for the cessation of all contraceptive protocols.”
Her eyebrows shot up, and she forced a thin smile. “A permanent Bond,” she mused, rising stiffly from her chair. She circled the desk, pacing around him slowly, her mind probing his hard, ignoring his clear discomfort. “An ambitious request, Captain. You seek to bond with a Quasar-class anomaly and sweep her away into a picture-perfect family life?”
“She is already a strong asset, Chancellor, I believe that—”
“You believe,” she interrupted, her voice soft. She stopped pacing in front of him. “Your belief is noted. But it seems like your reports are compromised by your… affection. Dr. Vaelith’s medical logs are similarly… biased.”
“I require a direct audit,” she whispered in his mind.
Darius physically recoiled like she had slapped him. “High Chancellor, I honestly don’t feel comfortable doing that considering—”
“The Valkyrie debt?” she bit out. She felt his body shiver, his mind panicking through the memories. The official story was a simple House trade infraction. The truth, which only she, he, and his grandfather knew, was financial treason. When Lord Titus Valkyrie had been in his prime, he had been caught embezzling trillions in raw materials from Forgedeep, diverting them to off-world black-market sites, effectively crippling their entire military production for decades. A crime he should have been executed for, but instead he sold off his mutt of a grandson to be an AAN strongman. She certainly didn’t see the mutt running off for a perfect life ever being a part of the deal. Him leaving with Nyx would leave a massive power gap in their force as well.
“If you refuse, then I will have no choice but to believe you are lying to me. Your House, your name, your history will all be formally investigated at the next public tribunal.”
He looked trapped, his jaw tight, and she could feel his fury, but at the end of the day he was a good soldier and always a responsible son. His shoulders slumped in defeat. “Very well, Chancellor,” he said, his voice tight with emotion.
“Very well… what?” she pushed.
“I agree to a direct audit.” His voice was tight with emotion.
Mereen nodded and turned her back to him, undoing the clasp of her gown with a snap. The starched fabric fell to the floor in a whisper of clothes, leaving her bare. She heard him shift behind her, standing and dropping his uniform trousers as she pulled up her terminal. Opening his medical reports from Rhea Vaelith, she began scanning through the contents, reaching out with her psionic subjugation, tearing through his mind.
Darius groaned; the intensity of her invasion would have crumbled a regular person, but being mixed-race he was able to endure it, barely. He entered her slowly, his massive size stretching her, his Magma energy searing through her body. She immediately dampened his energy, feeling his body cry out in pain from his internal temperature dropping so suddenly. He struggled to keep his pace as she sifted through his thoughts, his emotions, his most private feelings. He couldn’t lie, he couldn’t run, his energy allowing her a direct channel to the deepest parts of him. She felt his love for Nyx, his vulnerability, his hope.
“Such fervour,” she sneered aloud, “you truly care about this… anomaly.” She glanced behind her; the Ashdog was already slicked with sweat, his body shaking from the cold. He already knew Nyx’s name was Myth, but he didn’t know about her connection to the Dregs. Pitiful.
She was disappointed that he was blind to the danger lurking under his own nose, blinded by emotion and feelings, the same way his mother was. He flinched behind her as she forced him to relive the moment his grandfather got caught, the crushing weight of the truth, how easily he was traded. He picked up pace, his resentment surging through him, and she relished the renewed roughness of his pace.
A high-priority alert flashed on Mereen’s main console and she waved it away for it to immediately repopulate. She opened it with a deep sigh: a second attack, but in Aetherion. It wasn't too bad, even though it was their command centre; she knew Voxx and Nyx were already en route. She dismissed the alert and pulled up Nyx’s or Myth’s med report.
“Chancellor,” Darius grunted behind her, “I have to go if Aetherion is under attack—”
“Commander Voxx and Operative Nyx are already en route,” she said, a slow smile spreading across her face. She could feel him trembling physically, and she admired how strong his restraint was. Her mutation was formidable, but he could easily end her if he lost control. The thought sent a thrill through her core, but she kept her expression neutral, her eyes focusing on the terminal.
“You are a fascinating study, Captain,” her voice was flat. “So full of passion and duty, but so blind to the truths that lie right before you.” She surged against him, her energy flooding through his temporal lobe, his body shuddering under the weight. She moved her hips with brutal, mechanical precision, driving him deeper and harder. She could feel his resistance crumbling as the pleasure started building in spite of himself.
She could feel his body tensing, the full-body scans of Nyx on her terminal triggering a reaction in his exhausted brain. His muscles clenched as he desperately tried to hold back, but she poured her energy into him like a tidal wave, his pain feeding her pleasure, pushing her closer to the edge. His breath was ragged as he struggled with the tidal wave of sensations pouring out of her body, his hips lifting to meet her thrusts against his will.
She felt his desperate need to be loyal and she turned to see his eyes had darkened from their normal fiery orange to a dull brown. She dampened his energy more, causing his body to tense, his muscles clenching as he teetered on the brink of passing out. She released his energy just enough and, with a brutal thrust of her hips, sent him hurtling over the edge. His body convulsed on top of her as he struggled to get breath, his knees almost buckled under him. She didn’t stop until her own release crashed over her a heartbeat later.
As the final waves of pleasure faded, she stood up and returned to her seat, leaving him to pull his trousers on shakily. She felt as refreshed as he looked worn, and she frowned. “Captain. Your ear is bleeding.”
His hand went instinctively to his ear and he nodded silently.
“See the medical team here immediately. That’s an order. And your request for permanent Resonance Bonding is under advisement. As to the matter of your family’s continuing loyalty, I will confer with General Valkyrie to ensure there are no conflicts.”
He gave her a jerky nod and saluted stiffly, before walking out on unsteady legs.
Mereen watched him go with a cold flicker of satisfaction. She turned to the console, pulling up the feeds from both monster incidents, focusing on Aetherion. The city was being bled dry and her assets were in chaos.
She sighed heavily, fingers tapping absent-mindedly, before coolly redirecting the T.E.B. They were already spread thin, but it couldn’t be helped. She would have a discussion with Voxx once he returned; something was clearly very wrong.


























Chapter 31
Aetherion glittered beneath Nyx’s boots, the crystal walkways criss-crossing high above glowing gardens overflowing with brightly coloured flowers. At every corner, art installations shimmered between high-end storefronts. An opulent testament to the pinnacle of Aetherion beauty. People leisurely strolled to their destinations in flowing, brightly coloured clothes that fluttered delicately in the wind.
After they had seen Rhea again, Darius had wanted to show her his command deck in Aetherion. Where they would spend a lot of time whenever she was off light duty. It had been an imposing place, a hive of information and enforcers. They had picked up Synn Astra and Taryn Faye, both thrilled to have the opportunity to patrol with Nyx and Darius.
She walked between them, her boots clicking against the crystal loudly. She tried to maintain her calm exterior, but her mind was whirling. Darius had to leave midway through the patrol to meet High Chancellor Mereen. He said he hadn’t been scheduled until next week, but he supposed since he was on light duty and she probably had an open window, she had moved up the appointment. That had been almost two hours ago.
She checked her comtech for the fifth time that minute, her pulse skipping every time the screen came up empty.
“Almost to the last metre of our patrol,” Synn said, floating gently along beside her. “Then we can head back to headquarters.”
“Should we wait for Darius?” Taryn asked, her wild blue hair bouncing erratically.
Nyx forced a smile. “Let’s go back to headquarters after. Meetings are bound to run long considering everything that has happened with Fane and the Verge Convoy.”
Synn nodded in agreement. “They may want reinforcements as well. I don’t have the details, but it seems like Fane is putting up a good fight.”
Her heart sank; she could feel it in her bones, a gnawing sense of unease. She tried her comtech one more time before trying his private channel. A cold, automated reply floated across the screen:


CHANNEL RESTRICTED BY HIGH CHANCELLOR’S DIRECT AUTHORITY


Nyx felt the blood rush from her face and she slowly lowered her arm. She forced herself not to think as they continued their patrol. When they reached the summit of the Helix Spire, they began to finish their report. The beauty of the plaza gave her a sense of comfort as she looked down, the five kilometres to the surface of Titan obscured by a thick layer of clouds. The airborne city was a work of wonder, one of Titan’s crown jewels rivalling even Helion.
