He turned stiffly to look where Commander Voxx stood motionless. Voxx was covered in blood and grime, his face pulled taut. He didn’t move, just waiting silently as Mereen rapped her gavel for quiet.
“Order!” Her voice cut through the din. “Operative. As it seems you are without counsel, counsel will be provided for you. Until that can be arranged, you will be remanded to torpor emergency confinement. Dismissed.”
Darius half-turned, his ears picking up Eira's shrill voice above the din. “This was supposed to be her tribunal! They pulled us all here for this?!”
His mind churned; she was right. Something terrible was happening. Fane was still on the loose, an untold number of Dregs and monsters were in the Null Zone, and part of Titan was burning. He scanned the gallery, finally finding the face he was looking for. Ekros was smiling widely, waving his hands excitedly as he spoke to a set of T.E.B. officers. Darius felt his stomach churn as he tried to make his way over, but the imposing figure of General Titus Valkyrie blocked his path.
“We need to talk,” his grandfather barked over the din.
Darius nodded and followed his fiery back out into a side hallway, the sounds of the tribunal echoing away eerily.
“I saw you put in a request for permanent bonding with that traitor,” his grandfather sneered as soon as the door clicked behind them.
He gulped but stayed silent; there was nothing he could say that wouldn’t incriminate him more.
“I get word from the High Chancellor, not from you, or from that green medic girl. But the High Chancellor of Titan!” The General almost screeched.
Darius flinched involuntarily as the old man raised a fist at him.
“I will tell you this once, dog. You will neutralise this threat. Once and for all. Or I will do it myself.” His grandfather grinned wickedly. “And this time, I won't miss.”
Darius felt his jaw go slack as the General turned on his heel and stalked back into the tribunal. This time? His mind whirled as he strode hastily toward the medical centre of the complex. Helios and his grandfather had been on a joint mission years ago at the personal request of the High Chancellor. He had been a kid then, but he remembered them going to ‘deal with’ a Quasar-level flashcracking. There had been so much collateral damage that nothing in the area remained inhabitable, the land slowly becoming part of Nocturne. There had been no bodies left, and neither had reported the true death toll, but Darius knew it had been in the thousands. That area had been highly populated, and no evacuation warning had ever been recorded.
He shook his head as he rounded the corner, his eyes catching sight of Ekros. He felt his blood run cold and fire leap from his gauntlet as he strode to catch up with him.
“Ekros!” he roared, the doctor almost jumping out of his skin.
The old man turned and smiled warily at him. “Oh. Valkyrie, what can I do for you?”
“We need to talk,” Darius hissed. The doctor nodded once before jutting his chin towards a side door. Darius followed and watched him carefully as the man in the teal coat punched in a code, the door sliding open silently. Inside was already swarming with T.E.B. officers, all with weapons drawn and set to kill. They were finishing strapping down a very exhausted-looking Myth, carefully securing the heavy metal frame of the restraints around her. His stomach dropped, but he kept his composure, refusing to look into her eyes. The room quickly emptied except for the doctor and his captive, but Darius could see the faint, watchful eyes behind the viewing glass.
“What are you going to do? I have orders from—” he started, but Ekros cut him off with a giddy wave.
“Oh, shoo your orders. I am following the High Chancellor's orders, and her orders only. And she ordered me not only to put your partner under, but to understand her powers. Scientifically.”
Myth startled behind him, and he turned just in time to dodge a weak wave of energy. Before he had time to react, Ekros was already on her, a large needle stabbing deep into her neck. His hands were, for once, were ungloved and pale, wrinkled fingers were digging deep into her temples. Her eyes looked for help wildly, but Darius stood frozen to the spot. He watched in horror as Ekros’s powers flared, his own mind whirling in pain just from being in proximity to the old rat's psychic invasion.        `
He moved to interfere, but his grandfather’s words echoed through his head: “You will neutralise this threat. Once and for all. Or I will do it myself…”
Again.
Ekros was squinting at him, his other hand hovering over a bright red emergency call button. The old man wasn’t taking any chances and he knew if he pushed too hard now the T.E.B. would execute them both on the spot. 
He needed solutions. Looking one last time at Myth's tormented face, he turned and stalked out the door, his ears ringing as she screamed after him. His eyes stung as he picked up speed, sprinting down the hallway, his mind whirling. If he did anything other than let Ekros handle her, he would sacrifice everything. Fire and Magma poured off him in waves as he pulled up short at the viewing room. Commander Voxx stood outside, his shoulders slumped and his resonance so dim Darius almost didn’t notice him until he was a foot away.
Before he could speak, the haggard-looking man clamped him hard on the shoulder, pulling him the rest of the way into the corridor.
“I need you to go to the main entrance,” the Commander said earnestly, his voice low. “What you do there… it will decide everything.”
Darius frowned, torn between staying close by for Myth and following a direct order. “Sir—”
“That is an order, Captain.” Voxx’s hand was almost bruising on his shoulder.
“I can’t just leave my partner, sir! Not again!” Guilt flashed through Darius’s body, but he pushed it down. The look in Voxx's eyes made him hesitate. “What is happening at the entrance?”
“If you ever want to see Myth alive again, you will do as I say. Without you, she will die tonight.” The Commander straightened, an impassive mask falling over his features. “That is all, Captain.”
Darius nodded, slowly backing away before turning and running at full speed towards the stairs, his mind reeling. Myth. Had Voxx known who she was this whole time? The thought echoed through his head as he dropped down the open well of the stairs, his mutation roaring in response to the rapid rush of air. He couldn’t slow his thoughts, his mind racing as fast as he fell. What was happening at the entrance? The look in Myth’s eyes as he left burned in his brain, the look of complete betrayal. He felt the building vibrate slightly around him as he landed hard, immediately surrounded by blaring klaxons and warning sirens stuttering overhead.


E-E-EVACUATION PRO-P-PROCEDURES INITIATED: ALL PERSONNEL PLEASE EXIT IMMEDIATELY—


Darius turned just in time to shield himself with a thin wall of Magma before the entrance blew inward in a rain of glass, metal, and concrete debris. Aides and techs scurried around him, fleeing out the back exits and down stairwells. He squinted through the haze to see a mountain of a man wearing an Apex-scale mecha-suit in the courtyard. The ground around him was heaving unnaturally, massive fissures splintering up the front of the building, threatening the entire structure.
He felt his blood run cold as he shouted into his comtech, “Mayday! Mayday! Tribunal Complex entrance is under attack! Requesting immedi—”
A wall of flying metal slung him sideways into a pillar, his comtech shattering on contact. He felt a growl leave his throat, but welcomed the physical pain, focusing on the massive assailant. When had Dregs been so bold as to attack a high-security complex? He didn’t have time to ponder as he quickly dodged another barrage of metal and concrete, the building behind him shuddering as it began to sink backwards with a sickening crunch.
He felt his bones rattle as another seismic wave tore through him; his body, already exhausted from the day's events, fell sideways hard. His eyes caught a glimpse of a dark figure behind the giant before the figure was suddenly on him, the devastated, half-cyborg face of Vexal Ophedius inches from his own.
Before he could react, the insurgent's hand was around his neck, his lips inches from his ear. “Where is Myth?”
Darius stilled instantly, his jaw slightly agape. “Wha—” He felt a rush of something dark and necrotic wrap around his neck, his mutation sputtering wildly as a suffocating cold began to seep into his bloodstream. Rot.
“If you care at all about her, you will tell me where she is,” Vexal growled under his breath. “We both know she’s running out of time.”
Darius felt the blood drain from his face, his body finally going still. She was an insurgent. The thought screamed through him. He was shaking violently as he stumbled to his feet, the flames around his body only smouldering now under the crushing weight of Vexal's aura. The leash of Rot was still tight around his throat, and he struggled to speak. “The… the medical bay,” he finally stuttered. “But you will never reach her from the inside.”
“Noted.” Vexal turned, dragging Darius behind him like a ragdoll. “We will just scale the outside then.”
“The outside?!” Darius gasped as his body flew through the air, the insurgent racing up the side of the building beside him. Vexal was using powerful vines of Rot to scale the exterior, ash and debris raining down into the deep chasms opening up beneath the complex. The seismic giant’s damage was quickly becoming irreversible.
They were going to kill everyone here. He tried to flare his mutation, but a shockwave of necrotic pain flooded his senses.
“There’s no use struggling,” Vexal huffed beside him. “Tell me what floor she’s on and I won’t kill everyone in this sector.”
“Sixteenth!” he gasped, his body shuddering as he felt his internal temperature drop lower into a freezing void. “Please. Don’t hurt her.”
“Hurt her?” The Rot Saint’s voice was incredulous. “I am the only chance she’s got right now.”
Darius couldn’t control his face, the pain pouring through his body becoming unbearable. “Here! You will have to break through over ten feet of solid—”
The insurgent was already pouring his Rot into the wall of titanium and steel, purple and black tendrils snaking across the entire middle half of the building in a terrible, decaying web. Ash and liquified metal poured out of the structure, and Darius felt himself being pulled through the opening until a blinding light broke through the dark wall.
Voxx was standing at the command station, his massive wings extended, shielding the other Apexes from the explosion of shrapnel.
“Surrender the subject and pretty boy here lives,” Vexal’s deep voice boomed through the medical bay. Darius's vision was completely clouded over, but he could feel the Rot around his neck tightening as his feet dangled high above the courtyard below. He would have no way to recover from a drop this high in his weakened state.
I am going to die here…
“We will never surrender to a tyrant!” Mereen's cold voice rang out from the corner of the room. Darius squinted to take in Voxx, rings ready, but down on one knee as the Rot caved in the floor beneath him. He had never seen a mutation take over a space this rapidly, let alone bring the mighty Commander to his knees. Eira and Lynx were both lying on the ground, their bodies almost completely encased in Rot and shrapnel from the blast. Helios stood frozen to the spot, but Ekros was nowhere to be seen.
“Take her,” Voxx’s voice said coldly.
“Wise choice, old man,” Vexal sneered behind an ever-growing cloud of dark Rot.


Darius watched in horror, and with a foreign pang of relief, as the Rot immediately dissolved Myth’s bonds, the medical chair crumbling to dust beneath her. She looked beaten and torn up; part of her scalp was peeled away, her face bloody, and her arms clearly broken. He could feel Vexal’s raw, terrifying anger vibrating through the Rot and saturating the air.
By the time he registered what was happening, Vexal had him by the arm, his body dangling high above the vacant, collapsing courtyard. With a heave, Vexal hauled him back onto a solid ledge away from the crumbling wall. Alarms were flooding his senses, and the edges of his vision started to go black.
“Secure the perimeter now!” Mereen’s voice was shrill above the din. “I want every available officer looking for the asset. This is a Quasar-tier threat!”
Darius felt his body writhing from the pain, but he could feel nothing except a sinking feeling of dread as his eyes went completely dark, his world going still.
